> **DB-093 Rating-only reviews and empty replies / IN PROGRESS (2026-09-27), Backend & Integrations accountable with Frontend, Standard product lane, minor contract change:** Production (2026-09-27, review `c9703059`, reviewer trrohin): a star rating with no text was skipped by `generate_draft` without saying so (Regenerate AI returned in ~20 ms with nothing), the empty reply could still be approved (16:44:48Z), and the publish then failed as a generic 502 (16:44:49Z) because `publish_reply` refuses an empty reply. Separately, the Unreplied card read 5 while Needs reply listed 1: four reviews had just published successfully (`17015164`, `866adf2a`, `2d5992c2`, `12e17670`, ~0.5 s Google round trips) and the page refreshed the list but never the metrics. Changes: rating-only reviews get a draft from the star rating and first name alone, with an explicit instruction not to mention any visit details, a brief thank-you for 4-5 stars and an apology plus an invitation to contact the shop for 1-3 stars (brand voice, policy and the DB-088 dash rule still apply); `approve` and `publish` return 409 with a clear message for an empty reply; the page disables Approve and Publish while the reply is empty and refreshes the metrics after every action. The no-AI-key early return is unchanged. Existing rating-only reviews are not re-drafted automatically (DB-091 retries only failed drafts); one Regenerate AI click drafts them. **Contract note:** new 409s on approve/publish, Architecture sign-off owed under `AGENTS.md` rule 3. Evidence: backend 5 new tests (`tests/test_db093_google_reviews_rating_only.py`), 4 confirmed red first plus the no-key guard; frontend 3 new tests (`GoogleReviewsPage.emptyReply.test.tsx`) confirmed red first; Google reviews backend 47/47 across all seven suites, reviews frontend 29/29, `tsc` and `lint:changed` clean. Not Done.

> **DB-092 Google Reviews inbox: full history, dates, replied reviews read-only / IN PROGRESS (2026-09-27), Frontend & UX accountable with Backend, Standard product lane, contract change:** Product saw (2026-09-27) what looked like only unanswered reviews, no dates, and a replied review's text in the same editable box as an unsent draft. Worker logs show each sync reads 7 pages (~300-350 reviews) while `GET /google-reviews` returned only the newest 200 (`.limit(200)`), so the oldest replied reviews never appeared; the newest reviews happen to be the unreplied ones, so All looked like a to-do list. On a replied review, Save edit then Approve silently replaced the public reply, Regenerate AI overwrote the stored reply until the next sync restored it, and Publish was a no-op that still toasted "Reply published". Changes: the inbox takes `limit` (default 50, max 200) and `offset` and returns `{items, total, limit, offset}`, ordered newest first with `id` as tiebreak; the page loads 50 at a time with **Load more** and "Showing N of M", and accepts the old plain list so a frontend deployed ahead of its API still renders; list items show the review date and readable statuses (Replied, Awaiting approval); the detail panel shows review and reply dates; a replied review's reply is read-only under "Public reply on Google" with Regenerate AI and Publish hidden, and **Edit public reply** unlocks it with the warning "Approving will replace your current reply on Google."; `POST /google-reviews/{id}/generate` on a replied review returns 409. **Contract change:** inbox response shape and new query params, plus the 409; approved by Product in chat, Architecture sign-off owed under `AGENTS.md` rule 3. Evidence: backend 3 new tests (`tests/test_db092_google_reviews_inbox_history.py`) confirmed red first, DB-085's needs-reply test updated to the new shape; frontend 3 new tests (`GoogleReviewsPage.history.test.tsx`) confirmed red first; Google reviews backend 33/33, reviews frontend 26/26, `tsc` and `lint:changed` clean. Not verified in a browser: no connected Google tenant locally. Not Done.

> **DB-091 Retry failed Google review drafts, always behind approval / IN REVIEW (2026-09-26), Backend & Integrations accountable, High risk lane (changes the 15-minute sync worker):** Reported from production: after DB-084 fixed the model pin, the inbox still showed "AI draft unavailable: Error code: 404 ... claude-opus-4-1" on reviews. `sync_connection` drafted a review only when it was first stored (`elif created:`), and the failure reason is saved on the review, so every review imported while DB-078's `claude-opus-4-1` pin was live kept its stale 404 and an empty reply until someone pressed Regenerate AI by hand. **Change (`app/services/google_reviews_service.py`):** the sync also redrafts a stored review that is awaiting approval, has no reply text, and carries an "AI draft unavailable" reason. Product decided in chat (2026-09-26) that **retried drafts always require approval**: `generate_draft` gains `allow_auto_publish` (default unchanged), which the retry passes as False, so a retried 5-star draft never qualifies for the one-minute publish worker even with 5-star auto-publish on. First drafts of new reviews still follow the shop's setting. Automatic retries stop after 5 failed attempts per review (`ai_metadata.draft_attempts`), so a persistent failure cannot bill a model call every 15 minutes; Regenerate AI still works by hand. Reviews with a successful draft, an operator-written reply, a publish (not draft) failure, or a reply already on Google are never redrafted. No migration, API, or contract change. **Evidence:** `backend/tests/test_db089_google_reviews_retry_failed_drafts.py`, 9 tests; 4 fail against the previous service (retry happens and clears the error; retried 5-star draft is held for approval and absent from the publish worker's selection; a repeat failure records the new reason; retries cap at 5 -- 8 model calls over 8 syncs before the cap); 5 are guards that pass on both (4 never-redraft cases, and first drafts still follow auto-publish). Google Reviews suites 25/25. **Operational note:** on the first sync after deploy, each affected review costs one Opus 4.8 draft call; the screenshot showed about 8 reviews awaiting approval. **Gates owed (High risk lane):** independent QA and Security review, and a post-deploy check that the stale errors clear within one sync and that none of the retried reviews reach Google without approval. Not Done.

> **DB-089 Google connection health is computed but never shown / IN PROGRESS (2026-09-27; opened 2026-09-26 as DB-079, renumbered because DB-079 is the fleet PM item on main), Frontend & UX accountable, Fast UI lane:** `GET /google-reviews/connection/status` returns three health fields that no screen renders. `last_sync_at` and `last_sync_error` appear in the `Connection` type at `frontend/src/features/reviews/GoogleReviewsSettingsPage.tsx:8` and are never read again; `token_health` -- which the endpoint computes as `reconnect_required` / `healthy` / `not_connected` off `last_token_refresh_error` -- is not even declared. The inbox page references none of them. So the backend already knows the connection is unhealthy and the operator is never told: the reconcile task catches a failing sync and writes the reason to `last_sync_error` (`app/tasks/google_reviews.py:15`), the endpoint returns it, and the settings page drops it. Found while verifying DB-007 quota: with quota granted for the two location APIs but not the v4 reviews API, a shop would connect successfully, see a green **Connected** badge and its location name, and the inbox would stay empty forever with the explaining error sitting in the database. An expired refresh token fails the same silent way. The practical consequence is that **an empty inbox cannot today be distinguished from a failing sync** -- which is exactly the screenshot that opened this investigation. Acceptance: when `last_sync_error` is present the connection section shows it, in the same register as the existing `locationLoadMessage` amber block a few lines below; `token_health` of `reconnect_required` says so and offers the existing reconnect action rather than a bare error string; `last_sync_at` renders as a human "last checked" time, and its absence on a connected shop is itself worth showing, since it means the reconcile task has never completed once. Negative cases: a healthy connection shows no alarm; a disconnected or unconfigured shop keeps its current copy and gains no error; the amber block must not double up when a location-load error and a sync error are present together. Fast UI under `docs/DELIVERY_TEAM.md`: presentation only, no API field, response shape, or enum value added or altered -- every field already exists and is already returned -- and no migration. Prerequisite for trusting anything the Reviews UI says about connection state, but not itself a blocker on DB-007: it can land while quota is still 0, and would in fact be the thing that reports quota trouble from inside the app instead of the Cloud Console. **Implemented (2026-09-27, PR #437):** the connected state of `GoogleReviewsSettingsPage.tsx` renders a `ConnectionHealth` block: `token_health` `reconnect_required` shows an amber alert that the Google sign-in expired, with the existing **Reconnect Google account** action; otherwise a present `last_sync_error` shows "The last review sync failed: <reason>"; `last_sync_at` renders as "Last checked Google <local time>", and a connected shop with none is told the first sync runs within 15 minutes. Health renders only in the connected state, so a disconnected or unconfigured shop gains no error and the location-selection step keeps its single amber block. Evidence: 6 new tests in `GoogleReviewsSettingsPage.test.tsx`, 4 confirmed red first (last-checked time, never-synced notice, sync failure reason, expired sign-in with reconnect) plus 2 negative cases for the disconnected and location-error states; reviews frontend 20/20, `tsc` clean. Not verified in a browser: the local database has no connected Google tenant. Not Done.

> **DB-090 Google Reviews action feedback / IN REVIEW (2026-09-26), Frontend & UX accountable, Fast UI lane:** Reported from production: pressing **Regenerate AI** gave no sign it had been pressed while the model call ran for several seconds, inviting repeat clicks (a second paid call each) or action on the stale draft. `GoogleReviewsPage.tsx` now tracks an in-flight action per review: the pressed button reads "Generating…" / "Approving…" / "Publishing…" / "Saving…", that review's other actions are disabled and its reply box is read-only until the request settles, success or failure. Tracking is per review, so a second review can be worked (including its own generate) while the first is still generating, and a late response only replaces the panel if its review is still open. The reply label is now associated with its textarea. No API, contract, or backend change. **Evidence:** `frontend/src/features/reviews/__tests__/GoogleReviewsPage.generate.test.tsx`, 3 tests: pending label, disabled actions and read-only reply with a single request despite a second click; a second review stays usable and on screen while the first generates (fails with either the per-review scoping or the stale-response guard removed); actions release after a failed generate. 2 of 3 fail against the previous page (the third guards the new disable logic); reviews suites 16/16 pass; `tsc` clean. Browser pass on the worktree dev server with the real component and a 6-second stubbed generate: dark mode shows "Generating…" with actions dimmed, then the draft and a "Reply updated" toast; light mode confirmed via DOM (all three disabled at 0.5 opacity during the call). **Gap:** not a signed-in pass (local DB behind migrations, see DB-081).

> **DB-088 No em or en dashes in AI review replies / IN PROGRESS (2026-09-27), Backend & Integrations accountable, Fast lane (no contract change):** Product asked (2026-09-27) that AI-generated Google review replies never contain em or en dashes. Two layers: the draft system prompt now tells the model not to use them, and `_remove_dashes` in `google_reviews_service.py` cleans every generated draft before it is stored, so the rule holds when the model ignores the instruction. Numeric ranges become hyphens (`2–3 hours` -> `2-3 hours`), a trailing dash ends the sentence, every other dash becomes a comma, and plain hyphens are untouched. Scope is AI drafts only: text an operator types or edits is published as written. Evidence: 8 new tests (`tests/test_db088_google_reviews_no_dashes.py`), all confirmed red first (7 cleaning cases plus an end-to-end draft that also checks the prompt instruction); Google reviews backend suites 30/30 after the change. Not Done.

> **DB-086 Per-shop Claude model for Google review replies / IN PROGRESS (2026-09-26), Backend & Integrations accountable with Frontend, Standard product lane, contract change:** Product asked (2026-09-26) for a choice of AI model for reply drafts. Reply automation gains a **Reply model** select. The backend offers a fixed registry, `REPLY_MODELS` in `google_reviews_service.py`: `claude-opus-4-8` (default) and `claude-sonnet-5`; each entry carries its request shape, and Sonnet 5 runs with `thinking: {type: "disabled"}` because it thinks by default and thinking tokens would consume the 180-token draft budget, while Opus 4.8 runs without thinking when the parameter is omitted. The settings API validates `reply_model` as a `Literal` of the registry (unlisted or retired ids such as `claude-opus-4-1` are rejected), `GET /google-reviews/settings` returns `reply_model` and `reply_model_options`, and drafts record the model used in `ai_model`. **Contract change:** new request/response fields on `/google-reviews/settings` and migration `149_google_review_reply_model` (nullable `google_review_settings.reply_model`; NULL = platform default, so the default can change without a data migration). Approved by Product in chat; Architecture sign-off owed under `AGENTS.md` rule 3. Depends on DB-084 (#430): built on that branch because `main`'s DB-078 deny-list test still fails any `claude-opus-4-8` pin. Evidence: backend 6 new tests (`tests/test_db086_google_reviews_reply_model.py`), 4 confirmed red first, plus a rejection guard and a registry/`Literal` drift guard; frontend 1 new test confirmed red first; after the change backend reviews 15/15 plus 6/6, frontend reviews 11/11, `tsc` clean, alembic single head `149_google_review_reply_model`. Not verified: that this Anthropic account can call `claude-sonnet-5` in production -- choose it and Regenerate AI once to confirm. Not Done.

> **DB-087 Weekday-dependent PM test turns main red / IN PROGRESS (2026-09-27), Backend & Integrations accountable, Fast lane (test-only):** `test_fleet_workflows.py::test_completing_pm_rolls_date_and_mileage_forward` asserted `pm_due_date == today + 42 days`, but DB-079 (#425) rolls projected PM dates forward to the Saturday service day (`next_pm_service_day`). The assertion therefore held only when today + 42 was a Saturday, 1 day in 7; #425's CI ran on a Saturday and passed. It failed the required Backend full suite on #432 at 2026-09-27T01:53Z UTC (Sunday): `2026-11-14 != 2026-11-08`, 1 failed / 2077 passed. The production behavior is correct and unchanged; the assertion now applies `next_pm_service_day` like the neighbouring test. Evidence: failure reproduced in CI, weekday table shows the old assertion failing for 6 of 7 start days, fleet PM suites 91/91 locally. No production code change. Not Done.

> **DB-085 Google Reviews: respect existing replies, full history, reply filters / IN PROGRESS (2026-09-26), Backend & Integrations accountable with Frontend, High risk lane (writes to a public third-party surface):** The first production sync (50 reviews) treated every review as unreplied, though most already carried a public reply on Google. `upsert_review` ignored Google's `reviewReply`, so each review was queued for an AI draft, counted in "Unreplied 50", and left approve/publish free to **overwrite a reply the shop had already posted** (the v4 reply PUT replaces). The sync also read only the first page (no `nextPageToken`), and the inbox had no needs-reply view; the Unreplied card was inert. Changes: `sync_connection` pages through all reviews (`pageSize=50` + `pageToken`); a review carrying `reviewReply` is recorded as `published` with Google's reply text and update time and gets no AI draft, which also corrects the 50 already stored on the next 15-minute sync, except when an operator is mid-edit (awaiting approval with text differing from the AI draft) or a publish is in flight; `publish_reply` refuses to replace a Google reply unless a human approved the replacement; the inbox accepts `status=needs_reply` (new, awaiting approval, failed); the page adds a **Needs reply** tab, renames Published to **Replied** (it now covers replies written outside DieselBridge), and the Unreplied card opens Needs reply. **Contract note:** `needs_reply` is a new accepted value of the inbox `status` query parameter -- additive, approved by Product in chat 2026-09-26, Architecture sign-off still owed under `AGENTS.md` rule 3. No migration: `status` is a plain string column and no new status value is introduced. Evidence: backend 6 new tests (`tests/test_db085_google_reviews_existing_replies.py`), 5 confirmed red first plus one guard characterizing the operator-edit rule; frontend 3 new tests (`GoogleReviewsPage.filters.test.tsx`) confirmed red first; after the change backend reviews 16/16, frontend reviews 13/13, `tsc` clean. Known limit: the inbox list still caps at 200 rows (`.limit(200)`); Needs reply keeps actionable reviews visible. Until this deploys: do not approve or publish a review that already has a reply on Google, and keep 5-star auto-publish off. Not Done.

> **DB-084 Restore the Claude model pin (corrects DB-078) / IN PROGRESS (2026-09-26), Backend & Integrations accountable, Standard product lane:** **Correction to DB-078.** DB-078 replaced `claude-opus-4-8` with `claude-opus-4-1` at `google_reviews_service.py:15` and `description_library_service.py:30` on the claim that 4-8 was not a released model id. The reverse was true: `claude-opus-4-8` is a current model and `claude-opus-4-1` is retired. Production evidence: the first real review sync (worker, 2026-09-26T21:15:00Z) fetched 50 reviews with v4 `200 OK` and made 46 draft calls, all `POST /v1/messages 404 Not Found`; the inbox shows `not_found_error ... model: claude-opus-4-1` on each review. The description-library canonicalizer has been broken in production for the same reason since DB-078 deployed. Both pins restored to `claude-opus-4-8` -- kept rather than moved to Opus 5 because Opus 5 runs adaptive thinking by default and the draft call uses `max_tokens=180`, which thinking could exhaust before any reply text. DB-078's deny-list test (`KNOWN_BAD_PINS = {"claude-opus-4-8"}`) encoded the error and is replaced by a check against current model ids; both new assertions failed against `claude-opus-4-1` and pass after the change (10/10). No network in tests, so a real draft after deploy (Regenerate AI on one review) is the acceptance evidence. Existing failed drafts are not regenerated automatically. Not Done.

> **DB-083 Google Reviews inbox mobile overflow / IN REVIEW (2026-09-26), Frontend & UX accountable, Fast UI lane:** Found during the DB-081 (theme) browser pass. At 375px the Google Reviews inbox's review list card rendered 416px wide in a ~315px column, clipping each review's star rating off-screen. Cause: the list and detail panels are grid items with the default `min-width:auto`, so the `whitespace-nowrap` filter-tab row (414px) set the card's minimum width and its `overflow-x-auto` never engaged. Both panels in `GoogleReviewsPage.tsx` now carry `min-w-0`. Layout only; no API, contract, theme, or behavior change. The settings page was measured at 375px and has no overflow. **Evidence:** `frontend/src/features/reviews/__tests__/GoogleReviewsPage.layout.test.tsx` renders the inbox and asserts both grid panels are shrinkable -- fails without the change (panel classes lack `min-w-0`), passes with it; `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` clean. Browser pass on the worktree dev server with the real component and seeded data: at 375px page scroll width 347 equals client width, zero elements past the content edge, star ratings end at 341px inside the 345px edge, and the tab row scrolls within the card (414px content in 326px); at 1280px the panels keep the 1:1.1 split (494px / 544px) with no tab scrolling. **Gap:** not a signed-in pass (local DB is behind migrations, see DB-081); a signed-in phone-width route check remains for release.

> **DB-082 Google Reviews location save and account switch / IN PROGRESS (2026-09-26), Frontend & UX accountable with a Backend OAuth parameter change, Standard product lane:** The first real DB-007 connect after DB-080 reached the location list (three Business Profile locations returned, which also proves the production client secret) but every **Select** failed with "Could not save this location". Cause is a client/contract mismatch, not a deploy gap -- production was verified serving `main` `fb869783`: `GoogleReviewsSettingsPage.tsx` sent the `/connection/locations` item as-is (`{account_id, location_id, name}`) while `LocationSelection` in `google_reviews.py` requires `location_name` and forbids extra fields, so every save is rejected with 422. The page now sends `{account_id, location_id, location_name}`; the API contract is unchanged. Two account-switching gaps closed at the same time: the location-selection step had no way to use a different Google account (Reconnect showed only on errors or an empty list), now **Use a different Google account** is shown beside the list; and the authorization URL used `prompt=consent` only, which lets Google silently reuse the browser's signed-in account, now `prompt=select_account consent` so the account chooser always appears while a refresh token is still issued. Third fix, found while choosing the location: the picker listed the same shop twice ("Truck Pit Stop Truck & Trailer Repair" x2 plus "TruckPitStop"). Business Profile Manager shows the connecting account manages exactly one verified listing, 416 Seaboard Drive, Matthews NC, inside a location group named TRUCKPITSTOP; `list_locations` iterated every account the user belongs to (personal plus the group), and Google returns the listing under each. It now keeps each `location_id` once and requests `storefrontAddress`, and the picker shows the address (or "No storefront address (service-area listing)") under each name. **Contract note:** `/connection/locations` items gain an optional `address` field; additive, consumed only by this page, shipped in the same PR, but still an API field addition under `AGENTS.md` rule 3 and so owes Architecture review. Evidence: 5 new tests written first and confirmed red (payload shape, switch-account control, prompt parameter, duplicate listing -- `['111','111','222']` before the change -- and address display); after the change reviews frontend 9/9 pass, `tests/test_db078_google_reviews_activation.py` 10/10 pass, `tsc --noEmit` clean. Outstanding: Architecture review of the additive field, protected CI, merge/deploy, then the owner selects the location in production. Not Done.

> **DB-081 Google Reviews day/night theme / IN REVIEW (2026-09-26), Frontend & UX accountable, Fast UI lane:** `GoogleReviewsPage.tsx` and `GoogleReviewsSettingsPage.tsx` were written night-only (`text-white`, `text-gray-300/400/500`, `bg-white/5`, `bg-white/10`, `bg-black/20`, `border-white/10`, `border-white/15`, amber/emerald/red `-200/-300` notices). Under Day shop the inbox header link "Google connection & settings" rendered white on the road-white canvas, as did card labels and filter tabs; the settings page's notices and fields read light-on-dark. Both page roots now carry `db-reviews-workspace`, and `index.css` maps those utilities onto the appearance tokens (`--workspace-text/muted/border`, `--surface-overlay`, `--parts-status-warning/success/danger`) under `.db-presentation-new`, the same convention as the services, team and messages workspaces; the legacy presentation is untouched. No API, contract, or behavior change. **Evidence:** `frontend/src/features/reviews/__tests__/GoogleReviewsTheme.test.tsx` (4 tests) asserts both roots carry the workspace class and that every night-only utility either page uses is remapped -- 4/4 fail with the production change reverted, 4/4 pass with it; `npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` clean. Browser pass on the worktree dev server rendering the real components inside `db-staff-shell db-presentation-new` in light and dark (inbox with a selected review and failure text, settings not-configured and connected states, 375px compact): Day shop contrast measured at 14.54:1 for the settings link, 15.63:1 for filter tabs, 5.31:1 for card labels. **Gaps, stated plainly:** the browser pass was not signed in -- the local API 500s because the local Postgres is at `142_qbo_shop_activation` versus head `148` and those migrations add immutability triggers, so it was not upgraded for a CSS change; a signed-in route smoke check remains for release. `eslint src/features/reviews` reports 3 pre-existing `no-explicit-any` errors on lines this diff does not touch. Pre-existing, out of scope: at 375px the review list card overflows its grid column (filter-tab row widens the `min-width:auto` grid item), clipping the star rating.

> **DB-080 Google Reviews reconnect after a failed location load / IN PROGRESS (2026-09-26), Frontend & UX accountable, Fast UI lane:** Production dead end found during the first real DB-007 connect. When `/google-reviews/connection/locations` fails, `GoogleReviewsSettingsPage.tsx` showed only the error text and rendered **Reconnect Google account** solely when no locations loaded *and* no error existed, while **Disconnect** appears only once fully connected -- so a tenant whose stored refresh token had expired (Testing-mode 7-day expiry, connection from 2026-07-31) was told to reconnect with no control to do so. Production logs show `GET /connection/locations` 502 at 19:42:21Z and 19:42:22Z; the operator saw "Internal server error" rather than the endpoint's message because `app/main.py:308` replaces every 5xx detail. Fix: the page branches on status -- 503 keeps the quota-pending message with no reconnect (reconnecting cannot help), 502 shows an actionable expired-sign-in message with the reconnect button, and any other failure shows a fallback plus reconnect. Frontend only; no API field, response shape, or enum changes, and the global 5xx sanitization is deliberately left as is. Evidence: new `src/features/reviews/__tests__/GoogleReviewsSettingsPage.test.tsx`, 2 tests; the 502 test was run first and failed for the expected reason (page rendered "Internal server error" and no reconnect), the 503 test characterizes existing behavior; both pass after the change, `tsc --noEmit` clean. The page's hard-coded dark palette (unreadable under the day theme) is split to a separate session and not addressed here. Outstanding: PR, protected CI, merge/deploy, then the operator reconnect that completes DB-007 acceptance. Not Done.

> **DB-078 Google Reviews activation readiness / IMPLEMENTED, PR PENDING (2026-09-26), Backend & Integrations accountable, High risk lane:** The Google Business Profile review feature is fully implemented and deployed but dark, blocked on DB-007 (Google Basic API Access case `1-9174000041216`, quota 0). Two defects would have surfaced the moment quota was granted; both are fixed here and neither needed anything from Google. **(1) Invalid model pin.** `MODEL = "claude-opus-4-8"` is not a released Anthropic model id; it appeared at `app/services/google_reviews_service.py:15` and identically at `app/services/description_library_service.py:30`, so the description-library canonicalizer carried the same latent failure. Corrected to `claude-opus-4-1` at both call sites. **(2) Draft failures were invisible to the operator.** In `generate_draft` a model or API error is caught by a broad `except Exception` that recorded the reason only in `ai_metadata`, which `_serialize` does not return to the inbox -- so every failed draft reached the approval queue as an empty reply with no stated cause, while the publish path already surfaced its own failures via `publish_failure_reason`. The draft path now writes the same field, making the two paths consistent. Reusing that field exposed a second defect in the fix itself, caught before commit: the success path never cleared it, so a successful **Regenerate AI** would have left a stale red error sitting under a good draft. The success path now clears it. **(3) Zero test coverage.** No test anywhere in `backend/tests` referenced `google_review`, `google-reviews`, or `GoogleReview` across 178 files, for a module performing OAuth code exchange and refresh-token rotation, Fernet token encryption, tenant-scoped review storage, and authenticated writes to a third-party public-facing API. Adds `backend/tests/test_db078_google_reviews_activation.py`, 8 tests: both model pins, operator-visible draft failure, stale-failure clearing on successful regeneration, cross-tenant publish rejection, token encrypt/decrypt round-trip, decryption under a rotated key, and star-rating enum normalization. No test reaches Google or Anthropic over the network; the Anthropic client is replaced via `monkeypatch`. Not a contract change under `AGENTS.md` rule 3: no API field, response shape, or enum value is added or altered, and there is no migration -- `publish_failure_reason` is an existing column already returned by `_serialize`. Evidence: 4 tests written first and confirmed red for the expected reason (2 bad pins, 1 missing failure reason, 1 stale failure after successful regeneration), 4 characterize existing correct behavior; four independent mutations -- reverting the pin, deleting the failure-reason line, deleting the clear-on-success line, and removing the `cross-tenant review publish blocked` guard -- were each caught by exactly one intended test. `publish_failure_reason` was traced end to end: `_serialize` returns it and `GoogleReviewsPage.tsx` renders it in red, so the reason reaches the operator's screen rather than only the database. Explicitly out of scope: granting quota, setting production credentials, connecting any tenant. DB-078 makes activation safe; it does not perform it, and DB-007 remains Blocked on Google. Branch `codex/db078-google-reviews-activation-readiness` from `origin/main` `afb08566`. Backend full suite green on a clean environment: 2055 passed, 93 skipped, 0 failed (6m18s). Three runs isolate an unrelated pre-existing failure: pre-fix 1 failed/2053 passed, post-fix 1 failed/2054 passed (same single failure both sides, so no regression), and post-fix with the environment neutralized 0 failed/2055 passed. That failure is `test_db048_invoice_settlements::test_quickbooks_payments_provider_setting_is_dormant_and_nonmutating`, caused by `QUICKBOOKS_PAYMENTS_INVOICE_PAYMENTS_APPROVED=true` in the local `backend/.env` (line 55, dated 2026-09-12): it flips which gate trips first in `app/services/invoice_settlement_service.py:425-427`, yielding `quickbooks_payments_onboarding_incomplete` instead of the expected `quickbooks_payments_platform_approval_missing`. Confirmed against a clean `origin/main` worktree, where it passes. QuickBooks territory (DB-008), touching no file this item changes; local `.env` hygiene is worth a separate decision. CI's gating critical regression suite 82 pass, description-library suites 5 pass, `compileall` clean; CI runs no Python linter and no frontend file changed. MERGED AND DEPLOYED: PR [#424](https://github.com/mecaniser/truck-pit-stop/pull/424) passed all six required checks at job level on head `84317b7e` -- Migration graph 24s, Backend tests 1m8s, Frontend checks 2m30s, Frontend full suite 2m24s, Playwright smoke, and Backend full suite 10m52s reporting 2055 passed / 93 skipped / 0 failed, which independently confirms the QuickBooks failure was the local `.env` flag and not this change. Job-level conclusions were read rather than run status, since both "informational" jobs carry `continue-on-error: true` while remaining Required in branch protection. Squash-merged to `main` as `c34d2fe5` at 2026-09-26T17:49:20Z, branch deleted. Railway service `diesel-bridge-network` auto-deploys `main`, so the merge is the deploy: deployment `293a5107` built at 17:51:52Z and production now serves `c34d2fe5` (verified via `/build-version`, which matched the merge SHA exactly), `/health` returns `{"status":"alive"}`, and the reviews route answers 401 unauthenticated as before. Note the Railway deployment record still read `BUILDING` with a stale `updatedAt` after the build had finished -- the served artifact, not the status field, is the reliable signal. Deployed behavior is unchanged today and will stay so: DB-007 quota is 0, so no review syncs and no draft is generated. This ships insurance for activation day, not a live feature. Outstanding: independent QA and Security review for the credential and tenant boundaries this touches. Not Done.

> **DB-079 fleet board PM urgency, card identity and Saturday scheduling / IN REVIEW (2026-09-26), Frontend & UX accountable, Standard product lane:** Three board defects reported from the production screen, plus two requested changes. (1) A truck due today was filed under Maintenance to plan and rendered below trucks due in 7 and 8 days: the overdue test was `days < 0`, so 0 fell through to planning. `pmState` now classifies due-today as urgent and labels it "Due today" rather than "Due in 0 d"; it is the single place that decides this, so the card label and the section a truck lands in cannot disagree. (2) "PM soonest" read `pm_remaining` only, so a truck due by date sorted on its odometer and sank below trucks with weeks of road left; `pmUrgency` converts both axes to days at 600 mi/day and takes whichever fires first, with unscheduled trucks last. (3) Sections overrode the chosen order, so naming an explicit sort now collapses them into one list in that order while the default attention sort keeps its triage sections. (4) Cards flagged for a non-PM reason showed none; they now tag incidents and warning lights, excluding status and PM because the badge and PM bar already carry those. (5) The card leads with the company and lifts the unit number into the space beside the status badge at 34px; `fleetIdentity` splits the two from source fields and mirrors the backend's normalize rule so a unit already carrying its company is not printed twice. (6) PM work happens Saturdays, so every projection rounds forward to the first Saturday on or after the raw date - forward, not back, so a truck is never asked in sooner than its odometer supports - and an overdue truck books the next Saturday rather than today. The rule lives in one place per layer (`next_pm_service_day`, `nextPmServiceDay`) and the modal projects through the shared helper, so the date offered is the date the server stores. No API contract change: read-only presentation plus a changed default on an existing field. Two existing backend tests asserted the raw mileage arithmetic; their intent (days out, not months) is preserved and they additionally assert the rounded date never precedes the raw one. Evidence: every test written first and confirmed red; reverting the boundary fix alone fails the due-today cases. Backend 131 pass across the fleet suites, frontend 840 across 102 files, TypeScript clean, changed-source lint adds no findings (FleetModals pre-existing count 14 unchanged). Ordering verified in a browser against the production board's data shape; the Saturday rule verified through the running API container across every weekday start. Branch `codex/pm-urgency-sort` rebased onto main `c34d2fe5` after PR424 landed mid-flight. Outstanding: protected PR CI, independent QA, and signed-in browser acceptance on the real board. Not Done.

> **DB-038 stock value layout polish / REVIEW (2026-09-26), Frontend & UX accountable, Fast UI lane:** Follow-up to PR400 from a screenshot review of the shipped change. Existing contracts only; no API, migration, auth or data change. Branch `codex/db038-stock-value-polish` from main `afb08566`. Acceptance: (1) dollar figures on the Parts ledger row and At a glance group thousands via the shared exact-cents `payments/money.formatMoney` (Unit cost, Stock value, Selling price, header total), so `$1800.00` no longer sits beside `$1,800.00`; (2) the header stats line takes the header width instead of the 68ch prose cap, each stat is placed whole and wraps inside itself only when wider than a line, and a separating slash is never orphaned; (3) in the 761-1120px band multi-word ledger headers wrap to two lines instead of clipping (single-word Available still clips, unchanged); (4) the selected-part dismiss is hidden where the panel is docked (workbench over 80rem), where it only cleared a flag that layout never reads, and kept in the laptop sheet and phone views where it returns to the table. The laptop overlay itself is by design (`index.css`: the selected part is a dismissible sheet that preserves the ledger's scan width) and is unchanged. Evidence: tests written first and observed red; new unit case plus inventory/payments 191 pass; new Playwright case in the DB-038 spec, each of its five guards mutation-verified to fail when its fix is removed (68ch cap, plain inline stats, no-wrap stats overflowing Add Part at 390px, docked dismiss, header clipping); TypeScript and changed-source lint clean; browser pass at 1920/1000/800/390 on the mocked-API harness, not a signed-in session. Follow-up found, not caused here: two pre-existing DB-038 Playwright cases fail identically on main (a `Movement` tab now named Activity; Stock facts now 6, not 4); that spec is not in CI.

> **DB-038 parts stock value / RELEASED, GATES NOT CLEARED (2026-09-26), Frontend & UX accountable, Standard product lane:** Supersedes the 2026-09-16 IN PROGRESS entry below. [PR400](https://github.com/mecaniser/truck-pit-stop/pull/400) left draft and was merged by the product owner on 2026-09-16 as `c9549a7d`, with zero reviews. > **DB-076 forced update for long-lived tabs / RELEASED, QA PENDING (2026-09-24), Frontend & UX accountable, Standard product lane:** A fleet manager keeps the board open on an iPad for days. Deploys ship, but the tab keeps running old code and never asks for a new chunk, so nothing tells it to update. DB-074 does not cover this: its detection fires only when a lazy chunk 404s, and `features/fleet/FleetApp.tsx` imports `FleetBoard`, `TruckDetail`, `FleetMap` and its modals statically -- one chunk, already in memory. The manager can work the board indefinitely and never trigger it. API calls keep succeeding because the backend is deployed and compatible, so the app feels correct while running a week-old build. Acceptance: the build stamps a version the client can read and serves it at a path that is never cached; the client compares on an interval and on `visibilitychange` (the moment an iPad is picked back up -- `lib/sessionKeepAlive.ts` already uses that event); on mismatch with a clean idle page the app reloads silently, with no prompt (product decision, 2026-09-23); on mismatch with unsaved work it shows the DB-074 notice instead and reloads only when the person accepts; a release may additionally be marked breaking, which forces the reload after a visible countdown regardless of state. Negative cases: a failed or offline version fetch must never reload or nag; a version check must not fire while a form is dirty or a mutation is in flight; polling must not add meaningful load (single small request, backed off when hidden). Open questions for Architecture: what counts as "unsaved work" needs one shared definition (React Query mutation in flight plus dirty-form registry), and whether the version source is a static `version.json` written at build time or a backend field -- note `backend/app/main.py:682-715` serves both the API and the SPA, so either lands in the same service, and that same fallback is why a missing `/assets/*` path returns `{"detail":"Not Found"}` as JSON. RELEASED (2026-09-24): squash-merged as `4930fd19` at 00:19:08Z after all six required checks passed on `ee3d356b` (pinned with `--match-head-commit`). Auto-deploy fired normally this time -- `railway-app` registered a check suite and Railway began building 2s after merge. Verified against production, not inferred from Railway: `GET /build-version` returns `200 application/json` `{"sha":"4930fd19352087d656fed5e192c9c00db836b9de",...}` matching the merge commit, so `RAILWAY_GIT_COMMIT_SHA` reaches the Docker build and the stamp is not null; `Cache-Control: no-store`; `HEAD` 200. Pre-merge the same path returned `text/html` (SPA fallback). Live bundle `index-CjpXJxUI.js` contains the poll, the `visibilitychange` trigger and the `isMutating` guard. This deploy cannot demonstrate a reload by itself: open tabs adopt `4930fd19` as their running build, so the first silent reload happens on the NEXT deploy. Before merge, a new dirty-form test flaked in CI's frontend full suite; cause reproduced exactly (Modal takes focus on the next animation frame, which under load landed between click and first keystroke) and fixed by waiting for the shell to settle, a test-timing bug, not a product one. Earlier status: `/build-version` serves the stamp Vite emits, with `no-store` and GET+HEAD, registered before the SPA catch-all; the client adopts the first response as this tab's build and compares later polls, so a first poll never reloads and no sha needs compiling into client code (the `isLocalRuntimeServe` guard stays intact). Polls on an interval and on `visibilitychange`. Mutation count from `queryClient.isMutating()`. Two defects found while building, both fixed: the Dockerfile had no `ARG RAILWAY_GIT_COMMIT_SHA`, so the stamp would have shipped permanently null and the feature would have been silently dead; and the client had no way to learn its own sha, which the adoption pass now solves. Evidence: tests written first and confirmed red for the expected reason; backend 7, frontend 16 for this item, frontend 794 across 94 files; mutation-verified on the two paths that would destroy a manager's work (ignoring dirty forms, and treating a failed fetch as stale) plus the two contract requirements (no-store, HEAD). Endpoint verified against a running server: `no-store` present, sha matches the build, HEAD 200 while HEAD /robots.txt is 405. Browser-verified against a served build: the tab reloads itself with no prompt when the served sha changes, and does not reload at all when the endpoint returns 500 or returns HTML. Not Fast UI: app-wide runtime behavior, a new served endpoint, and a build-time stamp. Outstanding: observe the first real silent reload on the next deploy; the breaking-release countdown tier is NOT implemented; typing in any fleet Modal or SlidePanel now registers unsaved work (`useDirtyOnInput`, mutation-verified), but inline fields outside those two shells are not covered; protected PR CI; independent QA; and a signed-in browser pass on a real iPad. Today `index.html` returns no cache-control header and `/health` carries no build identity, so neither can answer the question as-is; the dev-only `VITE_DIESELBRIDGE_RUNTIME_SHA` is stripped from production builds by `isLocalRuntimeServe` in `frontend/vite.config.ts`. Depends on DB-074 (merged, deployed, `aa0a5ba7`) for the prompt UI it reuses. No branch, no implementation, no PR.

> **DB-075 PM calendar day load / IN REVIEW (2026-09-23), Frontend & UX accountable with a Backend contract addition, Standard product lane:** A manager rescheduling a PM could not see which dates were already busy, so trucks stacked onto one day while the next sat empty. **Contract change requiring Architecture review under rule 3:** new `GET /fleet/pm-day-load` returning `PMDayLoad` (`day`, `count`, `units`, `booked_count`, `booked_units`). Read-only over the existing `Vehicle.pm_due_date` and `Appointment.scheduled_at`; it schedules nothing, writes nothing, and adds no migration. Tenant-scoped, excludes deleted trucks, omits empty days, and rejects reversed or >366-day windows so month navigation cannot scan the whole fleet history. `include_repair_orders=true` additionally counts appointments, excluding cancelled and no-show; the two kinds stay separate because a day heavy with repairs is busy for a different reason than a day heavy with PMs. Semantics confirmed with the product owner: a PM count is trucks **due** that day, not trucks booked in - three due on a Saturday is the signal to move the fourth to the following week. Frontend: the shared `DatePicker` gains an optional `dayLoad`; busy days carry a count badge and tinted ground, and a reserved footer below the grid names the trucks. Three UI defects were found in-browser and each fixed test-first: an in-flow detail block pushed the grid down 97px and slid the hovered day out from under the pointer; as an overlay it covered the very weeks being compared against and took the pointer, causing a mouseleave/reopen flicker loop; and the trigger's hover drew a border inside the field's own border, reading as a double frame. Touch is a first-class case (the calendar is used on shop tablets): the first tap on a busy day reveals it, a second commits, tracked separately from hover because a tap fires mouseenter first. Acceptance: busy days are legible in light, dark and high_contrast; the footer hides no weeks and the grid never shifts; date-only values do not cross timezone boundaries; appointment bucketing is shop-local. Evidence: every test written first and confirmed red; tenant isolation verified by removing the filter, shop-local bucketing verified by reverting to UTC, both failed as intended. Backend 14 new tests, fleet suites 90 pass; frontend 761 pass across 92 files; TypeScript, changed-source lint and production build clean; verified in-browser at 420px and on an emulated iPhone 13 with touch (detail top 377 vs last day bottom 367, no overlap; grid top 187 identical idle and hovering). Branch `codex/pm-day-load` rebased onto main `7e6e2166` after PR409/PR410 landed mid-flight - the pre-rebase diff would have deleted the stale-deploy work, so the rebase was required, not cosmetic. Outstanding: Architecture contract review, protected PR CI, independent QA, and signed-in browser acceptance on the real screens. Local dev DB carries 8 seeded PM dates for demonstration, revertible via the recorded original values. Not Done.

> **DB-074 stale deploy recovery / IN REVIEW (2026-09-23), Frontend & UX accountable, Standard product lane:** Reported from production: `/dashboard/garage` showed a blank page, with the console reporting 404s for `MyGaragePage-BqMbfKJl.js` and other hashed chunks plus `Refused to apply style ... MIME type ('application/json')`. Root cause is not a code defect -- a deploy replaces every content-hashed asset, so a tab still running the previous build requests chunk filenames the server no longer has. Confirmed against production: `index-DlIE8I4u.js` and `index-C1FToTmH.css` (current `index.html`) return 200 while every asset named in the report returns 404, and a missing `/assets/*` path falls through to the API, which answers `{"detail":"Not Found"}` as `application/json` -- that fallback, not a stylesheet bug, produces the MIME refusal. The 401s on `/auth/me/appearance` and `/messages/unread-summary` are an unrelated expired session. Browser evidence showed React unmounts the entire tree when `React.lazy` re-throws a failed import and no error boundary exists, which is why the page is blank rather than partly working. Adds `lib/staleDeploy.ts` (detection across Chrome/Safari/Firefox wordings and the JSON-MIME symptom, a `vite:preloadError` and unhandled-rejection watch, and a `lazyRouteLoader` wrapper applied to all 31 lazy routes in `App.tsx` and `DashboardLayout.tsx`), `components/RouteErrorBoundary.tsx` (the app's first error boundary -- replaces the blank page and offers reload), and `components/StaleDeployNotice.tsx` for the non-render case, deduplicated via a `handled` flag so the message never appears twice. Not Fast UI: the boundary changes app-wide render-failure behavior, though no API, contract, migration, auth or tenant boundary changes. Acceptance: a dead route chunk shows one readable recovery card instead of a blank page; reload returns to a working app; an ordinary render error says "Something went wrong" and never claims a new version shipped; a non-stale rejection is ignored. Evidence: tests written first and confirmed red for the expected reason, with four independent mutations (removed listener, skipped teardown, ignored stale flag, over-broad pattern) each caught by exactly the intended test; frontend 740 pass across 92 files, up from 720/89, no regressions; TypeScript and production build clean; changed-source lint 0 findings across all 9 files. Browser-verified in Chromium against a served `dist` with a real chunk deleted and missing assets answering JSON 404, reproducing the reported console errors exactly: the unit tests passed while the real case still blanked, which is what exposed both the `React.lazy` unmount and a duplicate-message defect the unit tests could not see; after the fix the message shows once and clicking Reload recovers the app on the requested route. Branch `codex/stale-deploy-recovery` from main `f18867c1` (#408), candidate `0b15b1e8`, [PR409](https://github.com/mecaniser/truck-pit-stop/pull/409). Protected PR CI passed all five required checks -- Frontend checks 2m35s, Playwright smoke 2m55s, Backend tests 1m1s, Migration graph 28s, Frontend full suite (informational) 2m54s; Backend full suite (informational) still running. Merge state is BLOCKED on review only, not on a failing check. MERGED AND DEPLOYED: squash-merged to `main` as `aa0a5ba7` at 2026-09-23T16:12:30Z after all six required checks passed on `13d76f08` (Backend full suite 11m37s, Playwright smoke 3m2s, Frontend full suite 2m48s, Frontend checks 2m14s, Backend tests 1m4s, Migration graph 20s); `main` protection requires no approving review, so the six checks were the whole gate. Railway service `diesel-bridge-network` auto-deploys `main` and serves `www.dieselbridge.com`; CI validates `railway.json` but never deploys. Auto-deploy did NOT fire for this merge: GitHub emitted no `PushEvent` on `refs/heads/main` for `aa0a5ba7` and registered zero check suites on it, against 3 on the equivalent #408 merge commit `f18867c1` (`railway-app`, `cursor`, `github-actions`), so Railway was never told the commit existed. Railway config was unchanged throughout (`branch: main`, no staged changes, empty `pendingWork`), and `railway-app` did register on the branch pushes immediately before and after (`13d76f08`, `9dcafc41`), so the integration was healthy and this reads as a one-off GitHub event-delivery miss on that single commit rather than a broken connection. No manual deploy was triggered. The fix reached production instead by being swept along in later merges: `1c8996d0` (#414) at 17:52Z and `4f749ab6` (#415) at 18:04Z, the latter SUCCESS and currently live, with `aa0a5ba7` an ancestor of both. DEPLOY CONFIRMED against the live bundle `index-CF4Kx_Y2.js`: the notice and boundary copy ("A new version of DieselBridge is available", "Something went wrong", "Reload now", "Reload to finish") and all four detection paths (`dynamically imported module`, `preloadError`, `supported stylesheet`, `unhandledrejection`) are present, including the minified stale-vs-ordinary ternary. Correction: an earlier check in this item's history reported the fix absent from the live bundle. That was a faulty measurement -- 303KB of minified JS grepped through a shell variable rather than a file -- not a failed deploy; re-run against a saved file, every marker matched. Outstanding: independent QA, and a signed-in browser pass on the real staff shell -- the deploy evidence above is bundle content, not a person clicking through the deployed app. Note this fix could not demonstrate itself on its own deploy: it makes subsequent deploys recoverable, and the deploys that carried it handed already-open tabs the very error it fixes, now with a reload card instead of a blank page. This does not change the immediate remedy for the reported session: a hard reload. Unrelated untracked paths preserved. Not Done.

| DB-095 | P2 | Review — implementation and browser pass verified; delivery pending | State when parts last synced from Easy Truck Shop on the Parts workspace, and flag a stale sync | Frontend & UX | Fast UI lane, existing `/inventory/sync-status` contract — no API, data model, migration, auth, tenant, or dependency change. The Parts technical line reports TRACKED / NEEDS REORDER / OPEN PURCHASE ORDERS / STOCK VALUE, every figure of which is mirrored from Easy Truck Shop by the nightly sync, but the workspace showed no sync age: `PartsInventoryWorkspace` never called the endpoint, and the only indicator lived in `LegacyInventoryPage`, which `PartsInventoryGate` bypasses for garage_owner/garage_admin/receptionist. ### DB-035 controlled recovery

Product rejected local Adapt commit `5c8c698` as a product outcome: it preserved
behavior but substituted a rail/queue rearrangement for the connected operating
experience promised publicly. It is audit evidence only and is not eligible for
QA, integration or release. Recovery restarts from accepted Harden `a7b7fc0` on
`codex/db035-ux-recovery`. The replacement owner produced two isolated,
source-grounded prototype directions outside production source. Product now owns
the single topology decision recorded in `docs/DB-035_MASTER_EXECUTION_PLAN.md`;
no production implementation resumes before that decision.

## DB-032 acceptance criteria

- On the public landing page's first render, the header bridge constructs in a
  deterministic sequence—deck, pillars, then arch—while all twelve
  `DieselBridge` letters drop independently from varied heights and delays and
  settle at their existing final positions without layout shift.
- Every transformed letter box remains inside the header's vertical safe region
  and inside its reserved horizontal slot throughout the entrance, with no
  visible in-flight overlap at desktop or mobile widths.
- The motion is a one-time explanatory brand moment. It uses compositor-safe
  transform and opacity animation, an intentional ease-out movement curve,
  and no ambient loop, bounce, or repeated animation after data updates.
- Bridge construction is scoped to the animated header mark. The SVG box stays
  fixed while its paths animate; the footer wordmark, authenticated application
  logos, tenant logos, and all other brand surfaces remain static and unchanged.
- The accessible name remains `Diesel Bridge Network`; the animation introduces
  no duplicate announcement, focus change, interaction delay, or hidden content.
- Reduced-motion users receive at most a brief opacity transition with no
  positional travel. The final mark remains fully legible if CSS animation is
  unsupported or interrupted.
- Desktop and 390/320 mobile layouts retain their current dimensions, touch
  targets, and zero horizontal overflow. No API, auth, tenant, data, backend,
  dependency, worker, or migration change is introduced.
- Focused tests, full frontend tests, production build, changed-file lint/diff,
  and live browser inspection pass before independent QA.

## DB-015 acceptance criteria

- `/api/v1/ws` authenticates only from the existing HttpOnly `access_token`
  cookie. A query credential alone is rejected, and a query value can never
  override the cookie. No bearer/session token appears in the WebSocket URL.
- Browser handshakes require an exact match in the configured Origin allowlist.
  Authentication and authorization reuse the HTTPS legacy, selected customer-
  link, active-tenant, and WorkOS authorities. A WorkOS session requires an
  active, non-deleted `ExternalIdentity(provider="workos")`, an active linked
  principal, and an active, non-deleted
  `TenantMembership(provider="workos")` for the selected tenant. Its explicit
  provider role mapping must match the local `User.role`; an unknown role or
  any provider/local projection divergence fails closed initially and during
  live revalidation.
- Tenant-wide repair, invoice, payment, mechanic, and messaging events are
  available only to the explicit shop-staff roles `garage_owner`,
  `garage_admin`, `receptionist`, and `mechanic`. Driver, fleet-manager,
  platform, customer, and unknown roles never inherit that channel merely by
  being non-customer. A customer requires an exact active
  user/tenant/customer link and can register only on that customer channel.
- JWT expiry, blacklist, token version, user/tenant activity, customer link, and
  WorkOS membership remain valid for the life of the connection. Revocation,
  expiry, or a changed tenant context closes the socket with a generic reason.
- Existing server event payloads and `ping`/`pong` remain compatible. Client
  input is notification-only, size-bounded, and rejects unknown messages;
  connection limits and rate limiting remain enforced with generic close copy.
- Recursive redaction removes credential-shaped fields and query/URL values from
  structured application logs, observability context, persistent error records,
  exceptions, and repo-controlled Uvicorn access/error logging. Untrusted
  correlation headers are accepted only when they match the bounded safe-ID
  format; token-, JWT-, cookie-, overlong-, or control-character-shaped values
  are replaced before logging, echoing, or persistence.
- The frontend opens a queryless socket without reading the access token from
  JavaScript. `4001` may perform one HTTPS refresh recovery; authorization,
  origin, replacement, unsupported, and oversized closes are terminal, while
  rate limiting uses bounded backoff without a reconnect storm.
- No schema migration, ticket store, new secret, production data mutation, or
  event-contract change is introduced. Backend deploys before the queryless
  frontend to avoid a compatibility gap; rollback restores both halves together.
- Focused and full backend/frontend tests, compile/build, lint/diff, one Alembic
  head, authenticated browser coverage, secret-marker capture across console,
  API/proxy/observability logs, cross-tenant delivery denial, a fresh independent
  Architecture security GO, independent QA GO, CI, deploy, and canary are required.

## DB-004 acceptance criteria

- Authenticated customer portal dashboard, active repair, repair detail, estimate
  action, history list, and paid history detail render without clipping or hidden
  horizontal overflow at both 390px and 320px.
- The portal brand/home link, repair-detail back control, every history filter,
  and every other visible interactive target measure at least 44 by 44 CSS pixels
  at both required mobile widths.
- Portal routes expose an accurate customer-portal document title and never retain
  the staff-login title after authentication or client-side navigation.
- Customer login, active-repair/action-required state, detail/history navigation,
  keyboard focus and activation, unauthenticated denial, other-customer denial,
  and cross-tenant customer/order denial remain green.
- Focused portal tests, production build, changed-file lint/diff, and a real
  390/320 browser journey pass with no console, page, or failed-request errors.
  Independent QA must return GO before the focused PR can merge and release.

- At 390px and 320px, dashboard `Select & pay` and `Pay`, invoice-detail
  `Download PDF`, and `Review payment options` each measure at least 44 by 44
  CSS pixels; committed Playwright scrolls the full dashboard and invoice detail
  and measures every named control.
- Literal CSS pixel minimums remain effective under the compact portal root font,

## DB-029 acceptance criteria

- Remove the visible “Illustrative sample” and “Fictional repair-order data”
  disclaimer. Keep the example internally safe without adding visible legalistic
  copy, customer claims, testimonials, performance claims, or real PII.
- The curated rail order, labels, and Lucide icons are exactly Repair Orders /
  `ClipboardList`, Customers / `Users`, Shop Work / `Wrench`, Invoices /
  `FileText`, and Vehicle History / `History`. These are keyboard-operable tabs;
  selection replaces the entire miniature and is exposed semantically.
- Each miniature is faithful to its current product source rather than an
  invented generic dashboard: Repair Orders uses the repair-order workspace,
  invoice, work/labor, total, and history anatomy; Customers uses the real list,
  detail, Overview, and History patterns; Shop Work uses Shop Cockpit, Work Queue,
  Queue/Activity, and its three lanes; Invoices uses the embedded invoice-card
  states; Vehicle History uses vehicle details and Repair History rows.
- There is no global Intake/Estimate/Approval/Invoice/Payment stage rail and no
  module-by-stage Cartesian state. Each miniature exposes only authentic local
  controls: repair-order history/invoice/work evidence, customer Overview/History,
  Shop Work Queue/Activity and order cards, invoice selection/expansion, and
  vehicle-history row selection/expansion. Per-module local state is preserved.
- Fixtures are typed, deeply frozen, locally imported, internally reconciled,
  and use masked/safe values. Preview interaction performs no network request,
  auth/tenant read, WebSocket, storage write, or mutation and never mounts the
  authenticated production pages or their data hooks.
- The module route begins at the selected rail control and terminates at the
  context sheet. An event route appears only for an authentic selected row, card,
  disclosure, or history event and terminates at its evidence sheet. Routes and
  nodes never cross text, controls, or card interiors. Geometry comes from live
  rendered anchors, inflates obstacles by at least 8px, remeasures after resize,
  fonts, expansion, or content change, and suppresses any invalid route.
- The authored focal motion is the selected event travelling through its route
  into a capability-based morphing evidence sheet. Module-screen morph, glass
  depth, pointer-down feedback, focus, and CTA material motion stay subordinate,
  interruptible, and latest-selection-safe; there is no ambient loop or repeated
  generic rise. The CTA uses layered DieselBridge copper/orange material rather
  than a flat generic orange rectangle.
- Context and optional event sheets use functional Apple-style glass with clear
  edge, depth, translucency, hierarchy, and restrained specular response. If a
  module has no authentic secondary selection, the event sheet collapses rather
  than inventing evidence.
- Desktop keeps the product and its external evidence legible as one composition.
  At widths below 1200px routes are absent and evidence stacks in semantic order.
  At 390/320 the rail becomes a wrapping grid and authentic tables become compact
  rows/cards. The page has no horizontal overflow at 1440, 1366, 1280, 1120, 960,
  390, or 320 CSS pixels; visible interactive targets are at least 44px.
- Keyboard operation, focus visibility, reduced motion, reduced transparency,
  contrast, coarse-pointer behavior, 200% zoom, rapid retargeting, empty/optional
  data, late fonts, missing observers, and rotation/resize are verified without
  stale selection, focus, sheet, or route state.
- Dynamic replacement uses one visually hidden `role="status"` live region with
  polite, atomic announcements. A module change announces the selected module and
  screen; an authentic local selection announces its module, selected control or
  record, and evidence title. Initial render, focus-only movement, resize, and
  geometry/motion changes do not announce. A cancelable 120ms trailing update,
  guarded by the latest transition epoch, ensures rapid input announces only the
  final committed state with no stale or repeated intermediate message.
- Scope is frontend presentation only. No API, auth, tenant, payment, backend
  data, WebSocket, worker, route, or migration change is part of this outcome.
- Focused unit tests, full frontend tests, production build, lint/diff checks,
  source-fidelity and fixture invariants, responsive Playwright geometry and
  interaction coverage, independent QA, and an independent Impeccable/Emil
  finish review must pass before release.
- Release evidence must record the focused PR/merge SHA, required CI contexts,
  both Railway web deployment IDs and health gates, changed homepage canary, and
  proof that the worker deployment remained unchanged.

## DB-035 architecture contract and acceptance

- Scope covers the authenticated staff Dashboard, Customers, Repair Orders,
  Messages, My Shop, and Profile/Settings surfaces. The source-grounded public
  landing language informs hierarchy, typography, material, spacing, product
  identity, and motion; authenticated workflows remain source-faithful rather
  than becoming marketing replicas.
- **Shape confirmed 2026-08-12.** The new presentation is an operating-system
  restructure, not a cosmetic reskin: a permanent desktop product rail, a
  task-first Dashboard, and connected repair/customer/work/invoice/history
  context. The existing old/new presentation boundary remains the rollback
  mechanism; one router and the live operational data remain canonical.
- Rollout is controlled by a tenant/user-scoped old/new presentation flag with
  explicit precedence and instant rollback. Both modes use one router and the
  same routes, APIs, permissions, mutations, auth, tenant boundaries, business
  logic, WebSocket behavior, data, and operational/financial semantics. The flag
  may select presentation components or tokens only and must not fork domain
  state or introduce duplicate navigation trees.
- Architecture defines the flag owner, response shape, bootstrap behavior,
  caching, stale/offline fallback, tenant default versus user override,
  observability, compatibility window, and removal plan. Missing, malformed, or
  unavailable flag data fails safely to the current legacy presentation.
- DieselBridge remains the product identity in the authenticated shell. A tenant
  logo is subordinate shop context and cannot displace the product wordmark,
  navigation identity, accessible name, or browser/document identity.
- Appearance separates immutable brand/semantic tokens from personalization.
  User accent choices never recolor success, warning, destructive, financial,
  authorization, payment, or operational-risk states. Architecture publishes
  the token ownership map and prohibits component-local semantic overrides.
- Accent and theme choices come from a curated accessible set, not arbitrary
  colors. Every supported light/dark/high-contrast combination preserves text,
  icon, border, focus-ring, selected, disabled, and interactive-state contrast;
  forced-colors mode remains usable without relying on authored color alone.
- Appearance revisits accent, font family, font size, density, notification
  placement, theme/mode, live preview, reset, and persistence. Font choices are
  bounded, product-safe families. Compact, default, comfortable, and large are
  explicit type-and-density contracts with documented token values—not global
  transforms or browser-scale tricks.
- Every font/density combination preserves financial column alignment, table and
  work-queue comprehension, truncation/wrapping, 200% zoom, iPad/mobile layouts,
  and at least 44px visible interactive targets. Large type cannot shrink the
  available hit area or create horizontal page overflow.
- Appearance preference ownership is user-scoped server state for cross-device
  sync, with a small local bootstrap/cache for first paint. Architecture defines
  request/response fields, authorization, tenant isolation, validation/errors,
  concurrency or last-write semantics, cache versioning, hydration order, and
  failure behavior. No preference may be read from or written to another user or
  tenant.
- The compatibility plan inventories current localStorage appearance keys,
  defines a one-time validated migration into the server preference, prevents
  repeated overwrites, supports old clients during rollout, and removes obsolete
  keys only after the compatibility window. Reset restores the effective product
  or tenant default on every device and updates preview, cache, and server state
  consistently.
- Live preview is reversible and does not persist until the user applies it.
  Cancel restores the last committed appearance; reset and apply have explicit
  pending, success, error, and retry behavior. Notifications do not cover focal
  controls, move focus unexpectedly, or expose private preference payloads.
- The old and new presentations preserve semantic landmarks, headings, tab and
  disclosure behavior, labels, focus order, keyboard operation, live-region
  behavior, reduced motion, reduced transparency, high contrast, forced colors,
  coarse pointer support, and stable focus across flag or appearance changes.
- Acceptance covers every supported appearance combination on Dashboard,
  Customers, Repair Orders, Messages, My Shop, and Settings at desktop, iPad,
  390px, and 320px, plus 200% zoom, reduced motion, high contrast, and forced
  colors. The matrix verifies no overflow, overlap, hidden actions, undersized
  targets, broken tables, misaligned money, unreadable statuses, stale theme
  flashes, or notification obstruction.
- Representative Playwright journeys run in both old and new presentation modes
  against identical fixtures and assert identical routes, requests, permissions,
  mutations, and resulting business state. Appearance persistence is verified
  across reload, new tab, device/session bootstrap, user switch, tenant switch,
  offline/cache fallback, reset, and rollback.
- Architecture has recorded the versioned flag and preference contract, token
  map, localStorage migration inventory, fixtures, compatibility/removal plan,
  and work split. **Frontend & UX owns the confirmed reshape in
  `codex/db035-authenticated-presentation`**; independent Security reviews
  preference and tenant isolation, and independent QA plus Impeccable/Emil
  finish review gate the rollout.

## DB-HC001 acceptance criteria

- Every modified or untracked path is assigned to a coherent outcome, identified
  as generated/local-only material, or explicitly deferred without deletion.
- Branch and PR state is reconciled against GitHub; merged work is not reshipped
  and unmerged intentional work is not lost.
- Migration history has one Alembic head and the application starts against the
  expected schema.
- Backend, frontend, Playwright, security, tenant-isolation, payment/integration,
  and build checks required by the actual diff pass with fresh evidence.
- Independent Architecture, Security, QA, and pre-landing review gates are GO.
- Intentional work is committed in reviewable scope, pushed through PR and CI,
  merged, deployed, and verified with health/canary evidence and rollback notes.
- The primary repository ends clean. Remaining future work exists only as named
  board items, not unexplained local changes or ambiguous feature chats.

## DB-021 acceptance criteria

- Both Railway web services using root `railway.json` (`diesel-bridge-network`
  and performance `truck-pit-stop`) wait for HTTP `200` from `/health` before a
  new deployment becomes active and receives production traffic.
- A new instance has up to 300 seconds to complete legitimate startup; failure
  to become healthy in that window fails the deployment instead of replacing
  the last healthy instance.
- Automated coverage fails if the API health-check path or timeout is removed or
  changed, and `railway.json` validates against Railway's published JSON schema.
- There is no application behavior, API/data contract, migration, frontend, or
  worker deployment change in this item. Regression enforcement stays outside
  `backend/**` so the worker watch pattern is not triggered.
- Release evidence records both web deployment IDs/SHAs, successful Railway
  health gates, API health, and absence of connection-refused responses during
  each cutover; the worker is not redeployed.

## DB-070 acceptance criteria

Resolving a road incident currently sends `PATCH /fleet/incidents/{id}` with
`{status: 'resolved'}` and nothing else, so the fleet keeps no record of why an
incident stopped being a problem. The `resolution_notes` column, the
`IncidentUpdate` field, and the `IncidentResponse` field already exist and are
unused by the client; this item spends them rather than adding anything.

- Choosing Resolve from the incident action menu opens a panel asking what the
  outcome was. It does not resolve on the click.
- Submit is unavailable until a non-blank outcome is typed. Whitespace alone is
  not an outcome. The product owner chose free text over presets so the record
  reads as a sentence a person wrote, not a category someone clicked past.
- Submitting sends `status` and `resolution_notes` in one request; a failed
  request leaves the incident open and says so.
- The recorded outcome is readable after the fact, not write-only.
- No API field, response shape, enum value, migration, auth, or tenant change:
  this stays Fast UI only for as long as that remains true. Sending a field the
  contract already publishes is not a contract change; adding one would be.

## DB-071 acceptance criteria

`TruckDetail` filters incidents to `status !== 'resolved'` and hides the whole
section when none remain, so a resolved incident disappears from the only page
that showed it. The data is not lost — `GET /fleet/trucks/{id}/incidents`
returns it, and `GET /fleet/incidents/{id}/events` holds an append-only trail
that no client reads today — but the UI has no door to either.

- Truck detail shows a collapsed section for incidents that are no longer open,
  listing each with its outcome text and when it was resolved.
- Collapsed is the default. A resolved incident is history, and history must not
  compete with the unresolved card above it for a dispatcher's attention.
- Each incident can reveal its event timeline from the existing events endpoint,
  including who acted and when, with voided incidents distinguishable from
  resolved ones rather than merged into one bucket.
- Voided incidents appear here too. The backend already retains them; a record
  kept and never shown is the same as a record lost.
- No API, migration, auth, or tenant change. Both endpoints exist and are
  already authorized through the incident.

## DB-072 acceptance criteria

`FleetIncident.repair_order_id` can only be set by
`POST /fleet/incidents/{id}/create-repair`, which creates an order or appends to
the truck's open visit. Nothing can point an incident at an order that already
exists, so an incident repaired under an order opened by another route stays
unlinked forever.

This item adds an API capability and therefore is **not** Fast UI. Per
`AGENTS.md` rule 3 it routes to Architecture for a recorded contract before
backend and frontend proceed.

- Selectable orders are open repair orders **for the same vehicle** only. The
  product owner chose this over searching all orders: an incident pointed at
  another truck's order is a data-integrity defect that no UI affordance can
  undo, and closed orders cannot absorb the work anyway.
- Attaching an incident that already has a linked order is refused rather than
  silently reassigned.
- Attach and detach each append a `FleetIncidentEvent`, matching how
  `repair_order_created` is already recorded, so the link has provenance.
- Attempting to attach an order belonging to another vehicle, or to another
  tenant, is refused; the cross-tenant case returns the same generic
  not-found response the codebase already uses for foreign records rather than
  confirming the order exists.
- The existing `create-repair` behavior is unchanged, including its rule that an
  open visit absorbs the complaint instead of spawning a second order.
- Detaching an incident from its order must leave the order itself untouched.
- Contract, negative cases, and the tenant boundary carry test evidence before
  any UI consumes the route.

## DB-073 acceptance criteria

`DELETE /fleet/incidents/{id}` does not delete. It sets status `voided`, stamps
`resolved_at`, records a `voided` event, and refuses outright when a repair
order is linked. The menu calls this "Delete" in red, which tells a user their
record is gone when it is retained.

- The action and its confirmation read as voiding, not deleting.
- The confirmation says the record is kept for history rather than implying
  erasure.
- The existing refusal when a repair order is linked is surfaced as the reason
  it is, not as a generic failure.
- Behavior is unchanged; only the naming stops contradicting it. Pairs with
  DB-071, which is what makes the retained record actually reachable.

## DB-070 acceptance criteria

Resolving a road incident sent `PATCH /fleet/incidents/{id}` with
`{status: 'resolved'}` and nothing else, so the fleet kept no record of why an
incident stopped being a problem. The `resolution_notes` column, the
`IncidentUpdate` field and the `IncidentResponse` field already existed and were
unused by the client; this item spends them rather than adding anything.

- Choosing Resolve opens a panel asking what the outcome was. It does not
  resolve on the click.
- Submit is unavailable until a non-blank outcome is typed. Whitespace alone is
  not an outcome. Free text was chosen over presets so the record reads as a
  sentence a person wrote, not a category someone clicked past.
- Submitting sends `status` and `resolution_notes` in one request; a failed
